\displaystyle\quad 2\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/TraceDiagrams/T2.png}}}

\displaystyle\quad-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/TraceDiagrams/T3.png}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{d_{\chi}}\,

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}

\displaystyle\quad\left.\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/TraceDiagrams/T3b.png}}}\quad\right)

(79)

In the above diagrams, we use the following diagrammatic representation for the state
\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}
(in this particular case
\bar{A}_{2}=\bar{A}
):

\displaystyle\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}=\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/d_chi.png}}}

(80)

where
d_{\chi}=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}})=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}_{2}})
. This diagram will be used as a shorthand for
\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}
in all subsequent figures and contractions.

For the maximally mixed resource on
A_{2}
(so that
\chi_{A_{2}}=I_{A_{2}}/d_{\chi}

with
d_{\chi}:=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}})
), the corresponding resolvent appearing
in
D_{\ln}
simplifies as

\displaystyle\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}}+s\,I_{A_{1}A_{2}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\frac{I_{A_{2}}}{d_{\chi}}+s\,I_{A_{1}}\otimes I_{A_{2}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}}{d_{\chi}}+s\,I_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1}\otimes I_{A_{2}}\;:=\;\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s)\otimes I_{A_{2}}.

(81)

Here we have defined

\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s):=\left(\frac{\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}}{d_{\chi}}+s\,I_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1},

(82)

which is precisely the object denoted by
\Gamma(s)
in our trace-diagram notation. For the bulk contributions, the corresponding expressions involve an additional overall factor of
1/d_{\chi}
relative to the boundary case. Equivalently, at the level of the resolvent factors, this amounts to using a rescaled
\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s)
. The underlying reason is simple: in the bulk term we encounter the Fréchet derivative acting on
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}
rather than on
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}A_{2}}=\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}}
. Using the integral representation (
63
), the bulk contribution contains factors of the form

D_{\ln}\!\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\big)[Y]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+sI_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1}\,Y\,\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+sI_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1}\,ds,

(83)

for an operator
Y
supported on
A_{1}
(in our application,

Y=\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}(U)
).

It is convenient to express (
83
) in terms of the
same resolvent variable that appears in the boundary diagrams. Introducing

\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s):=\left(\frac{\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}}{d_{\chi}}+sI_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1},\qquad d_{\chi}:=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}})=\dim\chi_{A_{2}},

(84)

we use the identity

\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+sI_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1}=\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\,\Gamma_{A_{1}}\!\left(\frac{s}{d_{\chi}}\right).

(85)

Substituting (
85
) into
(
83
) and changing variables
s\mapsto s/d_{\chi}

gives

\displaystyle D_{\ln}\!\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\big)[Y]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+sI_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1}\,Y\,\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+sI_{A_{1}}\right)^{-1}\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s)\,Y\,\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s)\,ds.

(86)

Equation (
86
) explains the extra factor
of
1/d_{\chi}
in the bulk diagrams. For notational convenience, in the remainder of this section we write
\Gamma(s)
in place of
\Gamma_{A_{1}}(s)
.

Using the Weingarten calculus and the diagrammatic Haar-averaging rules collected in Appendix (
E.3.2
), we can now perform the
U
-integrals and obtain the expressions below.

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}\rangle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2d_{\chi}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\Bigg[

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{2}-1}\,\mathscr{R}_{1}(s)\,\Big(\tfrac{1}{d}\,W_{1}+\tfrac{1}{d}\,W_{3}-W_{2}-W_{4}-\tfrac{2}{d\,d_{\chi}}\,W_{8}+\tfrac{2}{d_{\chi}}\,W_{7}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{d^{2}-1}\,\mathscr{R}_{3}(s)\,\Big(W_{5}-\tfrac{1}{d}\,W_{6}-\tfrac{1}{d_{\chi}}\,W_{7}+\tfrac{1}{dd_{\chi}}\,W_{8}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{d^{2}-1}\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(s)\,\Big(\tfrac{1}{d}\,W_{2}+\tfrac{1}{d}\,W_{4}-\tfrac{2}{d}\,W_{4}-W_{1}-W_{3}+2W_{6}\Big)\Bigg].

(87)

where
W_{i}
and
\mathscr{R}_{i}(s)
are given in Table (
1
) in Appendix. A neat feature is that, once we average over the Haar random unitary, the contributions essentially separate: the
\mathscr{R}
terms depend only on the bulk state
\sigma^{0}_{A_{1}}
, while the
W
terms depend only on the skewing matrix
W_{R}
.

Now we explicitly perform the integral on s, and obtain the following functions of the spectrum:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\mathscr{R}_{1}(s,\lambda_{i})=\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\Tr(\sigma^{0}_{A_{1}}\Gamma(s))^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\frac{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}{(\lambda_{i}+s)(\lambda_{j}+s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\ln\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{j}}+\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}

(88)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(s,\lambda_{i})=\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\Tr((\sigma^{0}_{A_{1}}\Gamma(s))^{2})=1

(89)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\mathscr{R}_{3}(s,\lambda_{i})=\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\Tr((\sigma^{0}_{A_{1}})^{2}\,\Gamma(s))\Tr(\Gamma(s))=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\frac{\lambda_{i}^{2}}{(\lambda_{i}+s)(\lambda_{j}+s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{i}^{2}+\lambda_{j}^{2}}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\ln\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{j}}+\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}.

(90)

To simplify our notation, we use
f(\lambda)
to denote functions that depend on the entanglement spectrum
\{\lambda_{i}\}
and write
S(\lambda)=-\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\log\lambda_{i}
as the Shannon entropy. From these two expressions, define two functions
f_{1}(\lambda)
and
f_{2}(\lambda)
:

\begin{split}f_{1}(\lambda):=&\frac{1}{dd_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\left(\mathscr{R}_{3}(s,\lambda_{i})-\mathscr{R}_{1}(s,\lambda_{i})\right)\\
=&\frac{1}{2d}\sum_{i\neq j}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})\ln\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{j}}\\
=&-\sum_{i}\ln(\lambda_{i})/d-S(\lambda)\\
f_{2}(\lambda):=&\frac{1}{dd_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\left(\mathscr{R}_{3}(s,\lambda_{i})+\mathscr{R}_{1}(s,\lambda_{i})-d\,\ \mathscr{R}_{2}(s,\lambda_{i})\right)\\
=&\left(\frac{2}{d}+\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{1}{2d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j})^{2}}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\ln{\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{j}}}\right)-2.\end{split}

(91)

We specialize to the case where bulk qubits are a register of

n=\log_{2}d
qubits, each entangled with a reference qubit in
r
in a
parameterised Bell state.

|\Phi(\theta)\rangle=\cos\theta\,|00\rangle+\sin\theta\,|11\rangle,\qquad.

(92)

The joint bulk-reference pure state is

|\Psi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\rangle_{A_{1}r}=\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}\left(\cos\theta_{k}\,|0\rangle_{A_{1,k}}|0\rangle_{r_{k}}+\sin\theta_{k}\,|1\rangle_{A_{1,k}}|1\rangle_{r_{k}}\right),

(93)

with angles
\boldsymbol{\theta}=(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n})
. As discussed before, the resolvent representation of
D_{\ln}
requires the
relevant reduced states to be full rank. For the ansatz
(
93
), the reduced state on each bulk qubit has eigenvalues

\cos^{2}\theta_{k}
and
\sin^{2}\theta_{k}
, so full rank holds provided both are
nonzero. We therefore restrict to

0<\theta_{k}<\frac{\pi}{2}\qquad\text{for all }k=1,\dots,n,

which ensures
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}>0
and avoids the divergences associated with
zero eigenvalues. Tracing out the reference system
r
gives

\sigma^{(L)}\equiv\sigma^{0}_{A_{1}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\operatorname{Tr}_{r}\!\left(|\Psi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\rangle\langle\Psi(\boldsymbol{\theta})|\right)=\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}\begin{pmatrix}p_{k}&0\\
0&1-p_{k}\end{pmatrix},\qquad p_{k}:=\cos^{2}\theta_{k}.

(94)

Labeling the computational basis of
A_{1}
by bitstrings

b=(b_{1},\dots,b_{n})\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, the eigenvalues of
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}(\equiv\sigma^{(L)})
factorises as

\lambda_{b}=\prod_{k=1}^{n}\bigl[p_{k}\bigr]^{1-b_{k}}\bigl[1-p_{k}\bigr]^{b_{k}},\qquad d=2^{n}.

(95)

Now, let us define a pair of Hermitian matrices
J
and
D
as

\displaystyle J:=d_{\chi}\Tr_{\bar{A}_{2}}\left(\{W_{R},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\}\right)

(96)

\displaystyle D:=id_{\chi}\Tr_{\bar{A}_{2}}\left([W_{R},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}]\right)

which are diagrammatically represented in Fig.
97
,

\displaystyle J_{bj;ai}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MatrixP.png}}}\,+\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MatrixQ.png}}}\equiv\mathcal{W}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}+\mathcal{W}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}

(97)

\displaystyle iD_{bj;ai}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MatrixP.png}}}\,-\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MatrixQ.png}}}\equiv\mathcal{W}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}-\mathcal{W}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}

Here,
\mathcal{W}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}
and
\mathcal{W}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}
denote the respective contributions from the first and second trace diagrams. Then
\langle S_{corr}\rangle
can be reorganized into,

\begin{split}\langle S_{corr}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{2}\Big(c_{1}f_{1}(\lambda)+c_{2}f_{2}(\lambda)+c_{3}\Big)\end{split}

(98)

where we have defined:

\displaystyle c_{1}(J)=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{2d_{\chi}(d^{2}-1)}\Big(\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J^{2})-\frac{1}{d}\,\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}\right)

(99)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\,\Tr_{A_{1}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{2}}(J)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{dd_{\chi}}\,\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J)^{2}\Big)

(100)

\displaystyle c_{2}(D)=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{2d_{\chi}(d^{2}-1)}\Big(\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}((D)^{2})-\frac{1}{d}\,\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(D)^{2}\right)\Big)

(101)

\displaystyle c_{3}(D)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{\chi}(d^{2}-1)}\Big(-\frac{1}{d}\,\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}((D)^{2})+\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(D)^{2}\right)\Big)+2c_{2}

(102)

It suffices to show that the three coefficients
c_{1},c_{2}
, and
c_{3}
are strictly positive and that the functions
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are positive and monotonically decreasing in the bulk-entanglement parameter. Once these ingredients are established, the correction term satisfies
\langle S_{\text{corr }}\rangle\geq 0
and decreases as the bulk entanglement increases. The monotonocity and positivity of
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
has been shown in Appendix
C
. To show that
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
, and
c_{3}
are non–negative, we decompose
J
and
D
into orthonormal Pauli basis on
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
,

J=\sum_{a,b}p_{ab}\,P_{a}\otimes P_{b},\qquad D=\sum_{a,b}q_{ab}\,P_{a}\otimes P_{b},

with
\Tr(P_{a}P_{a^{\prime}})=d\delta_{aa^{\prime}}
and
\Tr(P_{b}P_{b^{\prime}})=d_{\chi}\delta_{bb^{\prime}}
, and all non–identity elements traceless. From this one directly obtains the trace combinations that appear in
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}
:

\displaystyle\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J^{2})

\displaystyle=dd_{\chi}\sum_{a,b}p_{ab}^{2},\qquad\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\bigl(\Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}\bigr)=d^{2}d_{\chi}\sum_{b}p_{0b}^{2},

\displaystyle\Tr_{A_{1}}\!\bigl(\Tr_{A_{2}}(J)^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=dd_{\chi}^{2}\sum_{a}p_{a0}^{2},\qquad\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J)=dd_{\chi}p_{00}

\displaystyle\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}((D)^{2})

\displaystyle=dd_{\chi}\sum_{a,b}q_{ab}^{2},\qquad\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\bigl(\Tr_{A_{1}}(D)^{2}\bigr)=d^{2}d_{\chi}\sum_{b}q_{0b}^{2}.

(103)

Substituting these expressions into the definitions of
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
, and
c_{3}
and simplifying yields

c_{1}=\frac{d^{2}}{2(d^{2}-1)}\sum_{a\neq 0,\,b\neq 0}p_{ab}^{2},\qquad c_{2}=\frac{d^{2}}{2(d^{2}-1)}\sum_{a\neq 0,\,b}q_{ab}^{2},\qquad c_{3}=\sum_{a,b}q_{ab}^{2},

(104)

so each coefficient is a positive constant times a sum of squares of Pauli coefficients, and thus

c_{1}\geq 0,\qquad c_{2}\geq 0,\qquad c_{3}\geq 0.

(105)

In Appendix
C
, we further show that the functions
f_{1}(\lambda)
and
f_{2}(\lambda)
are both monotonic decreasing function as we increase the entanglement of each bulk bell pairs defined in Eq. (
94
).
∎

A.3
Lemma
4.1

Proof.

The recovery is optimized by choosing a channel
R^{\ast}
that maximizes the coherent information of the logical-to-output map. For a fixed perturbation strength
\epsilon
, the encode–noise–recover procedure induces an effective quantum channel (cf. Eq. (
9
))

\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}:\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{L})\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}),\qquad\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\!\left(\sigma^{(L)}\right)=\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}},

(106)

where
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}}
is the recovered output state on the subsystem

A_{1}
. In our setup, Eq. (
9
) is specialized to
|\bar{A}_{1}|=0
and
\bar{A}_{2}=\bar{A}
. To quantify the performance of
R^{(\epsilon)}
, we evaluate the coherent information of
\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}
, defined in Eq. (
11
) as:

I_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}})=S\!\left(\Tr_{r}\!\left[(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\otimes I_{r})\bigl(|\Phi_{d}\rangle\langle\Phi_{d}|\bigr)\right]\right)-S\!\left[(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\otimes I_{r})\bigl(|\Phi_{d}\rangle\langle\Phi_{d}|\bigr)\right].

(107)

Figure 7:
Circuit picture for optimizing recovery via coherent information. A maximally entangled state

|\Phi\rangle_{Lr}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|i\rangle_{L}\otimes|i\rangle_{r}

purifies the logical input on
\mathcal{H}_{L}
, with
r
an isomorphic reference system.
The logical system is encoded by the isometry
V
, and the boundary region
A
is acted on by the recovery map
R_{A}
(with ancillary outputs
A_{1},A_{2}
), while
\bar{A}
is processed by
R_{\bar{A}}
.
This induces the effective channel
\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}:\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{L})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}})
, with output state

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}}=\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}(\sigma^{(L)})
.
The coherent information
I_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}})
is evaluated on the joint output

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}
and the full state
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A\bar{A}r}
, and is maximized over recovery channels to obtain the optimal recovery
R^{\ast}
.

Here
|\Phi_{d}\rangle
is a fixed maximally entangled state between the logical input system
\mathcal{H}_{L}
and an isomorphic reference system
\mathcal{H}_{r}
,

|\Phi_{d}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|i\rangle_{L}\otimes|i\rangle_{r},\qquad d=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{L})=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{r}),

(108)

with
\{|i\rangle_{L}\}
and
\{|i\rangle_{r}\}
orthonormal bases of
\mathcal{H}_{L}
and
\mathcal{H}_{r}
.

After encoding, perturbation, and recovery, the joint state on
A_{1}r
is

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}=\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\!\left[e^{i\epsilon W_{R}}\bigl(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\bigr)e^{-i\epsilon W_{R}}\right],

(109)

where
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\equiv|\Phi_{d}\rangle\!\langle\Phi_{d}|_{A_{1}r}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{r})
. The perturbation acts trivially on the reference, and we will therefore write
W_{R}
in place of
W_{R}\otimes I_{r}
when no confusion can arise.
Tracing out the reference yields the reduced output state on
A_{1}
,

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}}=\Tr_{r}\,\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}.

(110)

where
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\equiv\Tr_{r}\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}=I_{d}/d
. This admits the perturbative expansion

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}}=\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+\epsilon\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\,\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}}+O(\epsilon^{3}),

(111)

with

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}

\displaystyle=i\,\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\Big(\big[W_{R},\ \sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big]\Big),

(112)

\displaystyle\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big\{W_{R}^{2},\ \sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big\}-W_{R}\,\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big)\,W_{R}\Big).

(113)

In this notation, the coherent information of
\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}
takes the standard form

I_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}})=S\!\left(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}}\right)-S\!\left(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}\right).

(114)

We first expand
S(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}})
. Using the logarithm expansion in Appendix (
E
), we find

\displaystyle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle=S\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\big)-\epsilon\,\Tr\Big(\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon^{2}\,\Tr\Big(\,\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}}\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59596pt+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\big)\big[\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}}\big]\Big)+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(115)

Substituting the perturbations we obtain.

S(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}})=\ln d

(116)

and the correction vanishes. This is because
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{(0)}
is already maximally mixed.

We next evaluate
S(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r})
. A technical subtlety is that
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}=|\Phi_{d}\rangle\langle\Phi_{d}|
is pure, so a Taylor expansion of the entropy around
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}
is ill-defined because
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}
has zero eigenvalues. We therefore introduce a full-rank regulator family
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(\Delta)
on
\mathbb{C}^{d}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d}
,

\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(\Delta)=(1-3\Delta)\,|\Phi_{d}\rangle\langle\Phi_{d}|+\frac{2\Delta}{d(d-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}|ij\rangle\langle ij|+\frac{\Delta}{d-1}\sum_{m=1}^{d-1}|\Phi_{d}^{(m)}\rangle\langle\Phi_{d}^{(m)}|,\qquad 0<\Delta\ll 1.

(117)

and average its entropy over local unitary acting on
\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\simeq{\mathcal{L}_{a}}
. Here
\Delta
serves as a regulator. Sending
\Delta\to 0
recovers the pure state
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}
. The maximally entangled Fourier Bell basis is defined as

\ket{\Phi_{d}^{(m)}}\;=\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{d-1}\omega^{mk}\ket{k\,k},\qquad\omega=e^{2\pi i/d},\qquad m=0,1,\ldots,d-1.

(118)

The standard maximally entangled state corresponds to the
m=0
element of this basis,

\ket{\Phi_{d}}\;\equiv\;\ket{\Phi_{d}^{(0)}}\;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{d-1}\ket{k\,k}.

(119)

By construction, the reference extension is consistent with the original marginal on
A_{1}
, namely

\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}=\Tr_{r}\!\big[\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(\Delta)\big]=\frac{I_{d}}{d}.

(120)

Then we compute the Haar-averaged entropy of the perturbed state
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}(\Delta,U)
obtained after conjugating by a local unitary
U
on
A_{1}
.
Expanding
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}
perturbatively as in Eq. (
111
) (with
A_{1}\to A_{1}r
), we obtain the entropy expansion

\displaystyle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\big)

\displaystyle=S(\sigma_{A_{1}r}^{(0)}(U))+\epsilon\,\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon^{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)+\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\big]

\displaystyle\quad+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\big)\big[\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\big]\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{3}),

with

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)

\displaystyle=U\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\,U^{\dagger},

(121)

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)

\displaystyle=i\,\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\Big(\big[W_{R},\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big]\Big),

(122)

\displaystyle\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)

\displaystyle=-\,\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big\{W_{R}^{2},\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big\}-W_{R}\,\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big)\,W_{R}\Big).

(123)

To ensure the entropy admits a controlled Taylor expansion, the regulator must not be taken parametrically smaller than the perturbation strength; we therefore impose
\Delta\gtrsim\epsilon
. At the same time, we work in the regime
\Delta\ll 1
, so that
\Delta
remains small, justifying an expansion to leading order in
\Delta
.

Haar averaging over
U
removes all terms linear in
\epsilon
and the terms of the form
\Tr[\sigma^{(0)}D_{\ln}(\sigma^{(0)})[\cdot]]
, leaving

\displaystyle\Big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\Big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\big)\Big\rangle-\epsilon^{2}\int dU\;\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(U)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}r}(U)\big]\Big)+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(124)

Diagrammatically, Eq (
124
) can be represented as

\displaystyle\big\langle S\!\left(\sigma^{(R)}_{A_{1}r}\right)\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{d_{\chi}}\int dU\left(\frac{1}{2}\,\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/MISb2r.png}}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\left.\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/MISb1r.png}}}\,\,\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{d_{\chi}^{3}}\int dU\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\left(\,\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/T1br.png}}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-2\,\quad\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/T2br.png}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\left.+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/T3br.png}}}\,\right).

(125)

In these diagrams, the outermost loop (labeled by
r
) represents the reference Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{r}
.
We define the resolvent as

\Gamma_{A_{1}r}(s)=\left(\frac{\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}}{d_{\chi}}+s\,I_{A_{1}r}\right)^{-1}.

(126)

Integrating over
U
, we obtain the following expression in terms of the
W
- and
\mathscr{R}
-diagrams:

\displaystyle\Big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{d}{d_{\chi}(d^{2}-1)}\left(\mathscr{R}_{8}-\frac{1}{d}\mathscr{R}_{9}\right)\left(W_{5}-\frac{1}{d}W_{6}\right)

(127)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{d_{\chi}^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\;\frac{1}{d^{2}-1}\left(\mathscr{R}_{6}-\mathscr{R}_{7}\right)\operatorname{Tr}\left(W_{7}-\frac{1}{d}W_{8}\right)\Bigg\}.

For the regulated family
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(\Delta)
, the
\mathscr{R}
-terms in
Eq. (
127
) evaluates to

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{8}-\frac{1}{d}\mathscr{R}_{9}=

\displaystyle\Tr\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\right)-\frac{1}{d}\,\Tr\left[\Tr_{A_{1}}\left(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\right)\,\Tr_{A_{1}}\left(\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left((1-3\Delta)-\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right)\ln(1-3\Delta)+\left(\Delta-\frac{d-1}{d^{2}}\right)\ln\!\left(\frac{\Delta}{d-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(2\Delta-\frac{d-1}{d}\right)\ln\!\left(\frac{2\Delta}{d(d-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\Big(\mathscr{R}_{6}-\frac{1}{d}\mathscr{R}_{7}\Big)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\Tr\left[\Tr_{A_{1}}\left(\Gamma_{A_{1}r}(s)\right)\Tr_{A_{1}}\left((\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r})^{2}\ \Gamma_{A_{1}r}(s)\right)-\left[\Tr_{A_{1}}(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}\Gamma_{A_{1}r}(s))\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle dd_{\chi}\left((1-3\Delta)-\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right)\ln(1-3\Delta)+\left(\Delta-\frac{d-1}{d^{2}}\right)\ln\!\left(\frac{\Delta}{d-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(2\Delta-\frac{d-1}{d}\right)\ln\!\left(\frac{2\Delta}{d(d-1)}\right)

(128)

Now, substituting this back in the Eq.(
127
), and writing the
W
-diagrams in terms of
J
and
D
, we get expression
\Big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r};\Delta\big)\Big\rangle
, in leading order of
\Delta
, as following.

\displaystyle\Big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r};\Delta\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,\epsilon^{2}d}{d_{\chi}(d^{2}-1)}\,\Bigg(\frac{d-1}{d^{2}}\left[d\log\left(\frac{2}{d}\right)+(d+1)\log\left(\frac{\Delta}{d-1}\right)\right]\Bigg)\Bigg[\Big(\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J^{2})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{d}\,\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\,\Tr_{A_{1}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{2}}(J)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{dd_{\chi}}\,\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}((D)^{2})-\frac{1}{d}\,\Tr_{A_{2}}\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(D)^{2}\right)\Big)\Bigg]+O(\epsilon^{3},\Delta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\,\epsilon^{2}\,\Bigg(\frac{d-1}{d^{2}}\left[d\log\left(\frac{2}{d}\right)+(d+1)\log\left(\frac{\Delta}{d-1}\right)\right]\Bigg)\Big[c_{1}(J)+c_{2}(D)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+O(\epsilon^{3},\Delta).

The regulated coherent information is therefore

\displaystyle I_{c}^{(\Delta)}\big(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\big)

\displaystyle=\ln d-2\,\epsilon^{2}\,\Bigg(\frac{d-1}{d^{2}}\left[d\log\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)+(d+1)\log\left(\frac{d-1}{\Delta}\right)\right]\Bigg)\Bigg[c_{1}(J)+c_{2}(D)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad+O(\epsilon^{3},\Delta).

(129)

The
\Delta
-dependence of the
O(\epsilon^{2})
term is dominated by
\log\Delta
; all omitted contributions are
O(1)
or
O(\Delta)
as
\Delta\to 0
.

Finally, we optimize over the recovery. The prefactor multiplying
c_{1}(J)+c_{2}(D)
in Eq. (
A.3
) is strictly positive for
0<\Delta\leq 1/3
and
d\geq 2
, and the diagrammatic coefficients
c_{1}(J)
and
c_{2}(D)
are nonnegative (see Eq.
97
). Hence maximizing
I_{c}^{(\Delta)}
is equivalent to minimizing
c_{1}(J)+c_{2}(D)
over the allowed recovery operations.

Recall that the effective perturbation (conjugated by recovery unitaries) is

e^{i\epsilon W_{R}}=R_{A}^{(\epsilon)}\,R_{\bar{A}}^{(\epsilon)}\,e^{i\epsilon W}\,R_{A}^{(0)\dagger}\,R_{\bar{A}}^{(0)\dagger},

(130)

Composing the recovery with additional local unitaries,

R_{A}^{(\epsilon)}\mapsto e^{i\epsilon O_{A}}\,R_{A}^{(\epsilon)},\qquad R_{\bar{A}}^{(\epsilon)}\mapsto e^{i\epsilon O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}}\,R_{\bar{A}}^{(\epsilon)},

shifts
W_{R}
(to leading order in
\epsilon
) as

W_{R}\longrightarrow W_{R}+O_{A}\otimes I_{\bar{A}}+I_{A}\otimes O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}.

(131)

By the definition of the operators
J
and
D
(see Fig.
97
), this implies

J\longrightarrow J+2O_{A}+2I_{A_{1}}\otimes O^{\prime}_{A_{2}},\qquad D\longrightarrow D.

(132)

Thus local adjustments can change
J
but leave
D
invariant. In particular,
c_{2}(D)
is unaffected, whereas
c_{1}(J)
can be minimized by an appropriate choice of
O_{A}
(and
O^{\prime}_{A_{2}}
). For example, choosing
O_{A}=-\tfrac{1}{2}J
and
O^{\prime}_{A_{2}}=0
sets the shifted
J
to zero and yields the maximal value of
I_{c}^{(\Delta)}
within this class of local variations.

∎

A.4
Theorem
4.3

Proof.

We take the bulk input state in the pure-state case (cf. Eq. (
31
)) and express it in Schmidt form:

|\psi\rangle_{L}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\,|i\rangle_{a}\,|i\rangle_{\bar{a}},\qquad\lambda_{i}>0

(133)

Here, we denote

d=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}})
and
\bar{d}=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}_{1}})
.

In what follows, we assume
\bar{d}\geq d
so that the Taylor expansion of the entropy is well defined. The case
d>\bar{d}
can be recovered as a limiting case by taking some of the Schmidt coefficients to approach zero.

After skewed encoding and decoding the effective perturbation, the recovered state on the full physical Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{A\bar{A}}
is

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A\bar{A}}=e^{i\epsilon W_{R}}\Big(|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\otimes|\chi\rangle\langle\chi|_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\Big)e^{-i\epsilon W_{R}}.

(134)

In the unperturbed limit
\epsilon\to 0
, the recovered state factorizes as

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}

\displaystyle=|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}},

(135)

where we define

\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}:=|\chi\rangle\langle\chi|_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}},\qquad\chi_{A_{2}}:=\operatorname{Tr}_{\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\right].

(136)

The corresponding reduced unperturbed states are

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}A_{2}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{\bar{A}_{1}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}\right]=\operatorname{Tr}_{\bar{A}_{1}}\!\left[|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\right]\otimes\chi_{A_{2}},

(137)

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}\right]=|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}},

(138)

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{1}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}\right]=\operatorname{Tr}_{\bar{A}_{1}}\!\left[|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\right].

(139)

In the Schmidt basis of
|\psi\rangle_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}
, the reduced state on
A_{1}
is diagonal with eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{i}\}
, and hence admits the spectral decomposition

\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\,|i\rangle\langle i|_{A_{1}},

(140)

We now evaluate the Haar-averaged correction
\langle S_{\mathrm{corr}}\rangle
.
Since the unperturbed bulk state is supported on
A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}
, we average independently over local unitaries acting on each factor.
Concretely, the logical state is conjugated by local unitaries,

|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;(U_{A_{1}}\otimes V_{\bar{A}_{1}})\,|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\,(U_{A_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes V_{\bar{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}),

(141)

where
U
acts on
A_{1}
and
V
acts on
\bar{A}_{1}
, and then average over
U
and
V
with respect to the Haar measure. We emphasize that we do not average with a single global Haar unitary on
A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}
, because such a transformation would generically modify the entanglement structure between
A_{1}
and
\bar{A}_{1}
. By restricting to independent local Haar rotations, we randomize only local bases on each factor while preserving the intrinsic bipartite entanglement content of the bulk state. The resulting unperturbed rotated states are

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=(U\otimes V)\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\,(U^{\dagger}\otimes V^{\dagger})\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}},

(142)

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}A_{2}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{\bar{A}_{1}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)\right],

(143)

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{1}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)\right].

(144)

The corresponding skewed state on
A\bar{A}
is

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)=e^{i\epsilon W_{R}}\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)\,e^{-i\epsilon W_{R}},

(145)

with reduced states defined by partial tracing as in Eq. (
60
). Taking the expansion in
\epsilon
as Eq. (
62
), we obtain the Haar-averaged correction in the form

\displaystyle\langle S_{\mathrm{corr}}\rangle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\int dU\,dV\Bigg[

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\!\left(\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}A_{2}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}A_{2}}(U,V)\big)\!\left[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}A_{2}}(U,V)\right]\right)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{Tr}\!\left(\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}(U,V)\big)\!\left[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}(U,V)\right]\right)\Bigg].

(146)

while the first-order corrections are

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}A_{2}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=i\,\operatorname{Tr}_{\bar{A}_{1}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left(\big[W_{R},\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)\big]\right),

(147)

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=i\,\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{1}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left(\big[W_{R},\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}}(U,V)\big]\right).

(148)

Substituting (
147
)–(
148
) into
(
146
) and expanding the commutators into the resulting
trace terms using the diagrammatic convention and rules described in Appendix (
E.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle S_{\mathrm{corr}}\big\rangle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2d_{\chi}^{2}}\int ds\int dU

\displaystyle\left(\,-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/TraceDiagrams/T1pure.png}}}\right.

\displaystyle+2\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{d_{\chi}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}

\displaystyle\left.\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/TraceDiagrams/Tbk3pure.png}}}\,\right)

(149)

Applying the Weingarten calculus together with the diagrammatic Haar-averaging rules summarized in Appendix (
E.3.2
) yields

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2d_{\chi}d\bar{d}(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\Big[\,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{1}(s)\,\Big(2\widetilde{W}_{10}-2\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{9}-2d\widetilde{W}_{12}+2d\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{11}+2d\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{8}-2d\widetilde{W}_{5}-2\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{7}-2\widetilde{W}_{6}

\displaystyle-\left(1+d\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{2}+\left(d+\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{1}+\left(d+\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{3}-\left(1+d\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{4}+\left(d+\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{15}

\displaystyle-\left(1+d\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{13}-\left(1+d\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{14}+\left(d+\bar{d}\right)\widetilde{W}_{16}\Big)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{2}(s)\,\Big(2\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{12}-2\widetilde{W}_{11}-2d\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{10}+2d\widetilde{W}_{9}+2d\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{8}-2d\widetilde{W}_{5}-2\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{7}+2\widetilde{W}_{6}\Big)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{3}(s)\,\Big(2d\ \widetilde{W}_{10}-2d\bar{d}\ \widetilde{W}_{9}-2\ \widetilde{W}_{12}+2\bar{d}\ \widetilde{W}_{11}-2d\ \widetilde{W}_{8}+2d\bar{d}\ \widetilde{W}_{5}+2\ \widetilde{W}_{7}-2\bar{d}\ \widetilde{W}_{6}\Big)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{4}(s)\,\Big(-(d+\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{2}+(1+d\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{1}+(1+d\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{3}-(d+\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{4}+2d\widetilde{W}_{8}

\displaystyle-2d\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{5}-2\widetilde{W}_{7}+2\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{6}+2\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{10}-2\widetilde{W}_{9}-2d\bar{d}\widetilde{W}_{12}+2d\widetilde{W}_{11}+(1+d\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{15}

\displaystyle-(d+\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{13}-(d+\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{14}+(1+d\bar{d})\widetilde{W}_{16}\Big)\Big].

where
\widetilde{W}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{i}(s)
are given in Table. (
2
). They’re functions of the state on
A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}^{(0)}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}\ket{ii}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\bra{jj}.

(150)

Substituting this into the definition of
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}i(s)
and using

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\ket{i}\bra{i}=\sqrt{\sigma_{A_{1}}^{(0)}},

(151)

we obtain
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{1}(s)=\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{5}(s)=\mathscr{R}_{1}(s)
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{2}(s)=\mathscr{R}_{3}(s)
as functions of the eigenvalues
{\lambda_{i}}
. Equivalently, they are determined by the same spectral functions
f_{1}(\lambda)
and
f_{2}(\lambda)
, defined (as linear combinations) in Eq. (
91
).

\int ds\,\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{4}(s)=\int ds\,\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{6}(s)
is a constant function independent of entanglmeent spectrum, while
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{3}(s)
gives rise to a new spectra function
f_{3}(\lambda)
,

\displaystyle f_{3}(\lambda):=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d\bar{d}d_{\chi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{3}(s)

(152)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d\bar{d}d_{\chi}}\int ds\Tr\left(\rho\Gamma(s)\right)\Tr(\rho)\Tr\left(\Gamma(s)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d\bar{d}}\sum_{ij}\frac{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}{2(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})}\ln\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{j}}.

In analogy with Eq. (
96
), we define the following pair of Hermitian matrices:

\displaystyle J:=d_{\chi}\Tr_{\bar{A}_{2}}\left(\{W_{R},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\}\right)

(153)

\displaystyle D:=id_{\chi}\Tr_{\bar{A}_{2}}\left([W_{R},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}]\right)

Since
\bar{A}_{2}
is traced out, both
J
and
D
are supported on
A_{1}
,
\bar{A}_{1}
, and
A_{2}
. Their diagrammatic representations are shown in Fig.
154
.

\displaystyle J_{bj;ai}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/J2-diag-mixed.png}}}\,+\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/J1-diag-mixed.png}}}\equiv\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}+\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}

(154)

\displaystyle iD_{bj;ai}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/J2-diag-mixed.png}}}\,-\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/J1-diag-mixed.png}}}\equiv\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}

Here,
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}
denote the contributions from the first and second trace diagrams, respectively. Note that these quantities differ from
\mathcal{W}^{(1)}_{bj;ai}
and
\mathcal{W}^{(2)}_{bj;ai}
defined in Eq. (
153
), because of the additional contribution from
\bar{A}_{1}
.

Rearranging the terms and completing the squares, we obtain the following expression for the averaged
S_{corr}
:

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}\rangle=

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}(J)\rangle+\langle S_{corr}(D)\rangle.

(155)

where

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}(J)\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4d_{\chi}d\bar{d}(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}\epsilon^{2}\Bigg[\Big(d\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)-d\Tr\big(J^{2}\big)-\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)-\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\Big(d\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)-d\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{2}}(J)^{2}\big)-\bar{d}\Tr\big(J\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J)^{2}\big)\Big)\Big)df_{1}(\lambda)+\Bigg(d\Tr\big(Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)-d\bar{d}\Tr\big(J^{2}\big)+\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{d_{\chi}}\Big(d\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\big)-d\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{2}}(J)^{2}\big)-\Tr\big(J\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J)^{2}\big)\Big)\Bigg)d\bar{d}f_{3}(\lambda)\Bigg]

(156)

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}(D)\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4d_{\chi}d\bar{d}(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}\epsilon^{2}\Bigg[\Big(\Tr\big(Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\big)-\bar{d}\Tr\big(D^{2}\big)-d\Tr\big(\Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+d\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}}(D^{2})\big)\Big)df_{1}(\lambda)+\Big(-(1+d\bar{d})\Tr\big(Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\big)+(d+\bar{d})\Tr\big(D^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+(d+\bar{d})\Tr\big(\Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\big)-(1+d\bar{d})\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}}(D^{2})\big)\Big)f_{2}(\lambda)\ +\Big(-d\bar{d}\Tr\big(D^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+d\Tr\big(Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\big)-\Tr\big(\Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\big)+\bar{d}\Tr\big(Tr_{A_{1}}(D^{2})\big)\Big)d\bar{d}f_{3}(\lambda)\Bigg]

(157)

We now expand
J
and
D
in a product Pauli basis adapted to the tensor-factor decomposition.
Let
\{P_{r}^{(A_{1})}\}_{r=0}^{d^{2}-1}
and
\{P_{s}^{(\bar{A}_{1})}\}_{s=0}^{\bar{d}^{\,2}-1}
be Pauli (or generalized-Pauli) bases
on
\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}_{1}}
, respectively, and let

\{P_{t}^{(A_{2})}\}_{t=0}^{d_{\chi}^{2}-1}
be a Pauli basis on
\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}}
.
We choose these bases to be orthonormal with respect to the Hilbert–Schmidt inner product,

\Tr\!\left(P_{\alpha}^{(X)}P_{\beta}^{(X)}\right)=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{X})\,\delta_{\alpha\beta},\qquad X\in\{A_{1},\bar{A}_{1},A_{2}\},

(158)

With this notation,

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{d^{2}-1}\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{d}^{\,2}-1}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{\chi}^{2}-1}p_{lmn}(J)\,P_{l}^{(A_{1})}\otimes P_{m}^{(\bar{A}_{1})}\otimes P_{n}^{(A_{2})},

(159)

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{d^{2}-1}\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{d}^{\,2}-1}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{\chi}^{2}-1}p_{lmn}(D)\,P_{l}^{(A_{1})}\otimes P_{m}^{(\bar{A}_{1})}\otimes P_{n}^{(A_{2})}.

Since
\Tr_{A_{2}}(D)=0
, we have
p_{lm0}(D)=0
for all
l,n
.

Using the Pauli-basis coefficients of
D
, the trace expressions appearing in

\langle S_{\mathrm{corr}}(D)\rangle
can be written in terms of the following quadratic coefficient sums:

\displaystyle A_{D}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n>0}p_{00n}^{2}(D),\qquad B_{D}:=\sum_{m,n>0}p_{0mn}^{2}(D),\qquad C_{D}:=\sum_{l,n>0}p_{l0n}^{2}(D),\qquad E_{D}:=\sum_{l,m,n>0}p_{lmn}^{2}(D).

(160)

In terms of these coefficients, the corresponding trace expressions become

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\right)=d^{2}\bar{d}^{\,2}d_{\chi}\sum_{n>0}p_{00n}^{2}(D):=d^{2}\bar{d}^{\,2}d_{\chi}\,A_{D},

(161)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2}\right)=d\bar{d}^{\,2}d_{\chi}\left(\sum_{n>0}p_{00n}^{2}(D)+\sum_{l,n>0}p_{l0n}^{2}(D)\right):=d\bar{d}^{\,2}d_{\chi}\left(A_{D}+C_{D}\right),

(162)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(D)^{2}\right)=d^{2}\bar{d}\,d_{\chi}\left(\sum_{n>0}p_{00n}^{2}(D)+\sum_{m,n>0}p_{0mn}^{2}(D)\right):=d^{2}\bar{d}\,d_{\chi}\left(A_{D}+B_{D}\right),

(163)

\displaystyle\Tr(D^{2})=d\bar{d}\,d_{\chi}\left(\sum_{n>0}p_{00n}^{2}(D)+\sum_{m,n>0}p_{0mn}^{2}(D)+\sum_{l,n>0}p_{l0n}^{2}(D)+\sum_{l,m,n>0}p_{lmn}^{2}(D)\right)

(164)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad:=d\bar{d}\,d_{\chi}\left(A_{D}+B_{D}+C_{D}+E_{D}\right).

(165)

Similarly, we define the following quadratic coefficient sums of the Pauli-expansion coefficients
p_{lmn}(J)
:

\displaystyle A_{J}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n>0}p_{00n}^{2}(J),\qquad B_{J}:=\sum_{m,n>0}p_{0mn}^{2}(J),\qquad C_{J}:=\sum_{l,n>0}p_{l0n}^{2}(J),

(166)

\displaystyle E_{J}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,m,n>0}p_{lmn}^{2}(J),\qquad F_{J}:=p_{000}^{2}(J),\qquad G_{J}:=\sum_{m>0}p_{m00}^{2}(J),

\displaystyle H_{J}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{m>0}p_{0m0}^{2}(J),\qquad I_{J}:=\sum_{l,m>0}p_{lm0}^{2}(J).

In complete analogy, using the Pauli-basis coefficients of
J
, the trace terms appearing in

\langle S_{\mathrm{corr}}(J)\rangle
can be written as

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d^{2}\bar{d}^{2}d_{\chi}\left(A_{J}+F_{J}\right),

(167)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d\bar{d}^{2}d_{\chi}\left(A_{J}+C_{J}+F_{J}+G_{J}\right),

(168)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d^{2}\bar{d}d_{\chi}\left(A_{J}+B_{J}+F_{J}+H_{J}\right),

(169)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(J^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d\bar{d}d_{\chi}\left(A_{J}+B_{J}+C_{J}+E_{J}+F_{J}+G_{J}+H_{J}+I_{J}\right),

(170)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d\bar{d}^{2}d_{\chi}^{\,2}\left(F_{J}+G_{J}\right),

(171)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{2}}(J)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d\bar{d}d_{\chi}^{\,2}\left(F_{J}+G_{J}+H_{J}+I_{J}\right),

(172)

\displaystyle\Tr(J)^{2}

\displaystyle=d^{2}\bar{d}^{\,2}d_{\chi}^{\,2}\,F_{J},

(173)

\displaystyle\Tr\!\left(\Tr_{A_{1}A_{2}}(J)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d^{2}\bar{d}d_{\chi}^{\,2}\left(F_{J}+H_{J}\right).

(174)

Now we can expand
\langle S_{corr}(D)\rangle
in terms of these coefficients in a concise form:

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}(D)\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\left[\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\left(\bar{d}^{2}-1\right)C_{D}f_{2}(\lambda)+\frac{1}{2}\left(d^{2}-1\right)\bar{d}^{2}B_{D}\left(f_{2}(\lambda)-f_{1}(\lambda)\right)\right.

(175)

\displaystyle\left.+E_{D}\left(\frac{d^{2}\bar{d}^{2}}{2}f_{3}(\lambda)-\frac{d(d+\bar{d})}{2}f_{2}(\lambda)+\frac{d\bar{d}}{2}f_{1}(\lambda)\right)+const\right]/\left[(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)\right].

Similarly
\langle S_{corr}(J)\rangle
in terms of
J
is

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}(J)\rangle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\left[\frac{1}{2}d^{2}(\bar{d}^{2}-1)C_{J}f_{1}(\lambda)+E_{J}\left(\frac{d^{2}\bar{d}^{2}}{2}f_{3}(\lambda)-\frac{d^{2}}{2}f_{1}(\lambda)\right)\right]/\left[(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)\right].

(176)

Now define the following coefficients:

\displaystyle k_{1}:=\frac{d^{2}}{2(d^{2}-1)}C_{J},\qquad\qquad\quad\ k_{2}:=\frac{d^{2}}{2(d^{2}-1)}C_{D}

(177)

\displaystyle k_{3}:=\frac{d^{2}\bar{d}^{2}}{2(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}E_{D},\qquad k_{4}:=\frac{d^{2}\bar{d}^{2}}{2(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}E_{J}

\displaystyle k_{5}:=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}}{2(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}B_{D}.

The full
\langle S_{corr}\rangle
becomes

\displaystyle\langle S_{corr}\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\left[k_{1}f_{1}(\lambda)+k_{2}f_{2}(\lambda)+k_{3}\left(f_{3}(\lambda)-\frac{d+\bar{d}}{d^{2}\bar{d}}f_{2}(\lambda)+\frac{1}{d^{2}}f_{1}(\lambda)\right)\right.

(178)

\displaystyle\left.+k_{4}\left(f_{3}(\lambda)-\frac{1}{d\bar{d}}f_{1}(\lambda)\right)+k_{5}\left(f_{2}(\lambda)-f_{1}(\lambda)\right)+const\right].

Monotonicity of
f_{1}(\lambda)
,
f_{2}(\lambda)
and
f_{3}(\lambda)
are discussed in Appendix
C
.

Note that
f_{2}-f_{1}
is not a decreasing function of entanglement. Making the averaged PA entropy not monotonic for generic perturbation
W_{R}
. However, we show that non-monotonicity only occurs with small probability.

Assume that
W_{R}
is drawn from a Gaussian ensemble. In the product-Pauli expansion, we therefore model the Pauli-basis coefficients as independent random variables with zero mean and a common variance. Under this model, each quadratic block (e.g.
B_{D}
,
C_{D}
,
E_{D}
, and the corresponding blocks for
J
) is a sum of many squared coefficients, so its typical magnitude is proportional to the number of Pauli strings contributing to that block. Consequently, typical ratios of such blocks are governed by the ratio of the corresponding term counts, with fluctuations suppressed when the sums involve many terms.

We first estimate the typical ratios
B_{D}/E_{D}
and
C_{D}/E_{D}
by simple counting.
B_{D}
collects coefficients with the
A_{1}
-index fixed to the identity and with the remaining indices restricted to
m>0
and
n>0
. Hence the sum defining
B_{D}
contains
(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)
terms. Similarly,
C_{D}
fixes the
\bar{A}_{1}
-index to the identity and sums over
l>0
and
n>0
, and therefore contains
(d^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)
terms. Finally,
E_{D}
sums over
l>0
,
m>0
, and
n>0
, and thus contains
(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)
terms. Since each contributing squared coefficient has the same typical size, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\langle\frac{B_{D}}{E_{D}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}\approx\frac{(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)}{(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}-1},

(179)

\displaystyle\Big\langle\frac{C_{D}}{E_{D}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}\approx\frac{(d^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)}{(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}-1}.

Moreover, since
B_{D}
and
E_{D}
are sums of squares of Gaussian random variables, their normalized ratio
B_{D}/E_{D}
follows Fisher-Snedecor
F
distribution,

\frac{(B_{D}/N_{1})}{(E_{D}/N_{2})}\;\sim\;F_{N_{1},N_{2}},\qquad N_{1}=(\bar{d}^{\,2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{\,2}-1),\quad N_{2}=(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{\,2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{\,2}-1).

(180)

The non-monotonicity of
S_{PA}
requires the atypical fluctuation
B_{D}\gtrsim E_{D}
, i.e.
B_{D}/E_{D}\geq 1
. In terms of the
F
variable this corresponds to
F_{N_{1},N_{2}}\geq N_{2}/N_{1}=d^{2}-1
, and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{B_{D}}{E_{D}}\geq 1\right)=\mathbb{P}\!\left(F_{N_{1},N_{2}}\geq\frac{N_{2}}{N_{1}}\right)=\int_{N_{2}/N_{1}}^{\infty}\!dx\,f_{F}(x;N_{1},N_{2})\sim\mathcal{O}\!\left(e^{-\,d^{2}}\right).

(181)

We carry out the same counting estimate for the remaining
J
-term coefficients. Here
C_{J}
sums over
(l,n)
with
l>0
and
n>0
, while
E_{J}
sums over
(l,m,n)
with
l>0
,
m>0
, and
n>0
. Thus
C_{J}
contains
(d^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)
terms and
E_{J}
contains
(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)
terms, and hence

\Big\langle\frac{C_{J}}{E_{J}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}\approx\frac{(d^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)}{(d^{2}-1)(\bar{d}^{2}-1)(d_{\chi}^{2}-1)}=\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}-1}.

(182)

Substituting these typical ratios into the definitions of the coefficients
k_{i}
yields the corresponding estimates for the typical relative sizes. Using

\frac{k_{5}}{k_{3}}=\frac{d^{2}-1}{d^{2}}\,\frac{B_{D}}{E_{D}},\qquad\frac{k_{2}}{k_{3}}=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}-1}{\bar{d}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{D}}{E_{D}},\qquad\frac{k_{1}}{k_{4}}=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}-1}{\bar{d}^{2}}\,\frac{C_{J}}{E_{J}},

(183)

we obtain the corresponding typical GUE estimates by substituting the term-counting results for the block ratios.

\Big\langle\frac{k_{5}}{k_{3}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}=\frac{d^{2}-1}{d^{2}}\Big\langle\frac{B_{D}}{E_{D}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}=\frac{d^{2}-1}{d^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{2}-1}=\frac{1}{d^{2}},

(184)

\Big\langle\frac{k_{2}}{k_{3}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}-1}{\bar{d}^{2}}\Big\langle\frac{C_{D}}{E_{D}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}-1}{\bar{d}^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}-1}=\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}},

(185)

and

\Big\langle\frac{k_{1}}{k_{4}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}-1}{\bar{d}^{2}}\Big\langle\frac{C_{J}}{E_{J}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{GUE}}=\frac{\bar{d}^{2}-1}{\bar{d}^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}-1}=\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}}.

(186)

So in the large
d
and
\bar{d}
limit, we find that the typical PA entropy correction can be approximated as

\langle S_{corr}\rangle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}(k_{3}+k_{4})f_{3}(\lambda)+\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{d^{2}})+\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\bar{d}^{2}}).

(187)

∎

A.5
Lemma
4.2

Proof.

The overall encode–noise–recover process defines a quantum channel

\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}:\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{L})\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}),\qquad\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}(\sigma^{(L)})=\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}},

(188)

where
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}
denotes the recovered output state on
\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}
. In the present setting, the logical Hilbert space factorizes as

\mathcal{H}_{L}=\mathcal{H}_{a}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\bar{a}}.

(189)

Accordingly, we introduce two isomorphic reference systems
\mathcal{H}_{r}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{a}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\bar{r}}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\bar{a}}
.

To quantify the performance of the recovery channel, we consider the coherent information defined in Eq. (
11
) as

I_{c}\!\left(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\right)=S\!\left(\Tr_{r\bar{r}}\!\left[(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\otimes I_{r\bar{r}})\bigl(|\Phi\rangle\!\langle\Phi|_{a\bar{a}\,r\bar{r}}\bigr)\right]\right)-S\!\left[(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\otimes I_{r\bar{r}})\bigl(|\Phi\rangle\!\langle\Phi|_{a\bar{a}\,r\bar{r}}\bigr)\right].

(190)

where the maximally entangled state in Eq. (
190
) factorizes as

|\Phi\rangle_{a\bar{a}\,r\bar{r}}=|\Phi\rangle_{ar}\otimes|\Phi\rangle_{\bar{a}\,\bar{r}},

(191)

with

|\Phi\rangle_{ar}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{a}}}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{a}}|i\rangle_{a}\otimes|i\rangle_{r},\qquad|\Phi\rangle_{\bar{a}\,\bar{r}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{\bar{a}}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\bar{a}}}|j\rangle_{\bar{a}}\otimes|j\rangle_{\bar{r}},

(192)

and
d_{a}=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{a})
and
d_{\bar{a}}=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{\bar{a}})
.
When the logical system consists of
n
qubits, i.e.
\mathcal{H}_{L}\simeq(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\otimes n}
, one may view
|\Phi\rangle_{Lr}
as tensor product of
n
independent EPR pairs shared between the logical system and the reference:

|\Phi\rangle_{Lr}=\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(|0\rangle_{L_{k}}|0\rangle_{r_{k}}+|1\rangle_{L_{k}}|1\rangle_{r_{k}}\right),

(193)

so that
n_{a}
Bell pairs are shared between
a
and
r
together and
n_{\bar{a}}
Bell pairs are shared between
\bar{a}
and
\bar{r}
(with
n=n_{a}+n_{\bar{a}}
for qubits).

Figure 8:
Channel picture for optimizing recovery via coherent information in the factorized logical setting
\mathcal{H}_{L}=\mathcal{H}_{a}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\bar{a}}
. The logical input is purified by a product of maximally entangled states
|\Phi\rangle_{ar}\otimes|\Phi\rangle_{\bar{a}\bar{r}}
, with
r\simeq a
and
\bar{r}\simeq\bar{a}
.The joint logical state
\sigma^{(L)}_{a\bar{a}}
is encoded by the isometry
V
into
\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}}
, followed by recovery maps on each boundary region. This induces an effective channel
\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}:\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{L})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}})
, with output
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}=\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}(\sigma^{(L)}_{a\bar{a}})
. The coherent information is evaluated on the joint outputs
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}r}
and
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{\bar{A}_{1}\bar{r}}
(and the full state
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A\bar{A}r\bar{r}}
), and maximized over recovery channels to obtain the optimal recovery
R^{\ast}
.

After encoding, perturbation, and recovery, the joint state on the recovered subsystem
A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}
and the reference
r\bar{r}
is

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}=(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\otimes I_{r\bar{r}})\bigl(|\Phi\rangle\!\langle\Phi|_{a\bar{a}\,r\bar{r}}\bigr)=\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[e^{i\epsilon W_{R}}\bigl(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\bigr)e^{-i\epsilon W_{R}}\right],

(194)

where
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}
is the density matrix of the input maximally mixed state

|\Phi\rangle_{a\bar{a}\,r\bar{r}}
. The perturbation acts trivially on the reference systems, so we adopt the shorthand

W_{R}\otimes I_{r}\otimes I_{\bar{r}}\;\equiv\;W_{R}.

(195)

Tracing out the reference in Eq. (
194
) gives the reduced output state on
A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}
,

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}=\Tr_{r\bar{r}}\,\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}.

(196)

In this notation, the coherent information is

I_{c}\!\left(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\right)=S\!\left(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\right)-S\!\left(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\right).

(197)

Maximizing
I_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}})
over the choice of recovery unitary defines the optimal recovery, denoted by
R^{\ast}
, which preserves the largest possible amount of recoverable bulk information in the decoded outputs
A_{1}
and
\bar{A}_{1}
.

Let’s first calculate entropy
S(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}})
, using the perturbative expansion

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}=\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}+\delta\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}=\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}+\epsilon\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\,\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(198)

where

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\equiv(U\otimes V)\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\,(U^{\dagger}\otimes V^{\dagger})

(199)

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=i\,\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A_{2}}r\bar{r}}\Big(\big[W_{R},\ \sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\big]\Big),

(200)

\displaystyle\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=-\Tr_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}r\bar{r}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big\{W_{R}^{2},\ \sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}}\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-W_{R}\,\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\big)\,W_{R}\Big).

(201)

Using the logarithm expansion derived in Appendix.
E
, we find

\displaystyle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big)

\displaystyle=S\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big)-\epsilon\,\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\epsilon^{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}(U,V)\big]\Big)+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(202)

Similar as in Sec.
A.3
, this entropy becomes

S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\big)=S\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\big)=\ln(d\bar{d}).

(203)

and correction vanishes.

We next compute the entropy of the joint recovered-and-reference state,

S(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}})
.
\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}
admits the perturbative expansion

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}=\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}+\delta\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}=\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}+\epsilon\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\,\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(204)

where

\displaystyle\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\!\left[\sigma^{(0)}_{A\bar{A}\,r\bar{r}}(U,V)\right]\equiv(U\otimes V)\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\,(U^{\dagger}\otimes V^{\dagger}),

(205)

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=i\,\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\Big(\big[W_{R},\ \sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\big]\Big),

(206)

\displaystyle\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)

\displaystyle=-\operatorname{Tr}_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big\{W_{R}^{2},\ \sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-W_{R}\,\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\otimes\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\big)\,W_{R}\Big).

(207)

The derivation proceeds exactly as before, with the only change being that the entropy expansion is now carried out on the enlarged Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{r}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\bar{r}}
.
Accordingly, every instance of the zeroth-order reduced state in the previous computation is replaced by

\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}
, and the perturbation generator is taken to act trivially on the reference systems,

W_{R}\mapsto W_{R}\otimes I_{r}\otimes I_{\bar{r}}
.
With these replacements in place, we obtain

\displaystyle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)

\displaystyle=S\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)-\epsilon\,\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\epsilon^{2}\,\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(208)

A technical point arises at this stage. The Fréchet-derivative term

D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\big)\Big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\Big]

appearing in
\langle S(\sigma_{A_{1}\tilde{A}_{1}r\tilde{r}}^{(e)})\rangle
given in Eq (
211
)is ill-defined because
\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}
is a pure state and hence not full rank. In particular, it has zero eigenvalues, so the resolvent

(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}+sI)^{-1}
diverges as
s\to 0
.

We handle this by introducing a full-rank regulator at the level of the Bell-pair factorization (
193
).
Specifically, for each Bell pair state, denoted as
\ket{\Phi_{+}}
, we replace the projector
|\Phi^{+}\rangle\langle\Phi^{+}|
by the following state

\bigl(|\Phi^{+}\rangle\langle\Phi^{+}|\bigr)^{\mathrm{reg}}=(1-3\Delta)\,|\Phi^{+}\rangle\langle\Phi^{+}|+\Delta\Bigl(|01\rangle\langle 01|+|10\rangle\langle 10|\Bigr)+\Delta\,|\Phi^{-}\rangle\langle\Phi^{-}|,

(209)

where
|\Phi^{\pm}\rangle=(|00\rangle\pm|11\rangle)/\sqrt{2}
.
The regulator is introduced at the level of the input purification.
The regulated output state of the channel is then obtained by pushing this input state through the channel
\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}

\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(\Delta):=\bigl(\mathcal{N}_{R^{(\epsilon)}}\otimes I_{r\bar{r}}\bigr)\!\left(|\Phi\rangle\langle\Phi|^{\mathrm{reg}}_{a\bar{a}\,r\bar{r}}\right).

(210)

We evaluate
S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\big)
by first performing the perturbative expansion at fixed
\Delta>0
and then taking the limit
\Delta\to 0
at the end of the calculation.

Note that Eq. (
203
) is still valid since

\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}(\Delta)=I_{d}/d
and

\sigma^{(0)}_{\bar{A}_{1}}(\Delta)=I_{\bar{d}}/\bar{d}
,
which remain maximally mixed even in the presence of the regulator.

Then we compute the Haar-averaged entropy
\big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)\big\rangle
. Averaging the entropy expansion over
U
and
V
with respect to the Haar measure gives

\displaystyle\Big\langle S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=S\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}\big)-\epsilon^{2}\int dU\,dV\;\operatorname{Tr}\Big(\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,D_{\ln}\big(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big)\big[\delta^{(1)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+O(\epsilon^{3}).

(211)

We now focus on the first
\epsilon^{2}
-order term in Eq. (
211
). This term alone is sufficient to determine the PA entropy when the channel-capacity optimization condition is imposed. The key point is that the PA entropy in Eq. (
176
) depends only on the Pauli coefficients
p_{rst}(J)
(defined in Eq. (
166
)) with
t>0
. By contrast, after carrying out the average over
U
and
V
, one finds that the second
\epsilon^{2}
-order term in Eq. (
211
) depends only on the sector
p_{rs0}(J)
with
t=0
. Furthermore, the first term which we compute below (see Eq. (
A.5
)), receives decoupled contribution from the
t>0
and
t=0
sectors, so the optimization conditions for
t>0
coefficients are determined independently of the
t=0
ones. It is therefore enough to optimize the first term to fix the optimal
t>0
components and hence the PA entropy.
We denote this contribution by
\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
, defined as

\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}:=-\epsilon^{2}\int dU\,dV\;\operatorname{Tr}\left[\delta^{(2)}\sigma_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\,\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}r\bar{r}}(U,V)\right].

(212)

Diagrammatically,
\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
takes the following form

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{d_{\chi}}\int dU\left(\quad\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/MISb2r1pure.png}}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/MISb2r2pure.png}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/MISb1r1pure.png}}}

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[height]{theory_draft_images/MI_diagrams/MISb1r2pure.png}}}\quad\right).

(213)

In the diagrams above, we have used the identity for the logarithm of a tensor-product state

\ln\bigl(\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes\sigma^{(0)}_{\bar{A}_{1}}\bigr)=\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}}\otimes I_{\bar{A}_{1}}+I_{A_{1}}\otimes\ln\sigma^{(0)}_{\bar{A}_{1}}.

(214)

After averaging over the Haar measures of
U
and
V
and substituting the regulated marginals

\sigma^{(0)}_{A_{1}r}(\Delta)
and
\sigma^{(0)}_{\bar{A}_{1}\bar{r}}(\Delta)
, the expression simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle=\frac{3(1-4\Delta)\,\epsilon^{2}}{4d_{\chi}}\,\log\Bigg(\frac{1-3\Delta}{\Delta}\Bigg)\Bigg[\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{9}(\log d+\log\bar{d})}{d\bar{d}}+\frac{\bigl(d\,\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{9}-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{11}\bigr)\,\log d}{\bar{d}\,(d^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\frac{\bigl(\bar{d}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{9}-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{10}\bigr)\,\log\bar{d}}{d\,(\bar{d}^{2}-1)}\Bigg]

(215)

Using Table (
2
) together with
Eqs. (
168
), (
169
), and (
170
), we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{9}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Tr(J^{2}+D^{2})=\frac{d\bar{d}d_{\chi}}{4}\Big(A_{J}+B_{J}+C_{J}+E_{J}+F_{J}+G_{J}+H_{J}+I_{J}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+A_{D}+B_{D}+C_{D}+E_{D}\Big)

(216)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{10}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Tr(\Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(J)^{2}+\Tr_{\bar{A}_{1}}(D)^{2})=\frac{d{\bar{d}}^{2}d_{\chi}}{4}\Big(A_{J}+C_{J}+F_{J}+G_{J}+A_{D}+C_{D}\Big)

(217)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Tr(\Tr_{A_{1}}(J)^{2}+\Tr_{A_{1}}(D)^{2})=\frac{d^{2}\bar{d}d_{\chi}}{4}\Big(A_{J}+B_{J}+F_{J}+H_{J}+A_{D}+B_{D}\Big)

(218)

Now we get

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\epsilon^{2}}{16}(1-4\Delta)\,\log\Biggl(\frac{1-3\Delta}{\Delta}\Biggr)\Biggl[\frac{d^{2}\log d}{d^{2}-1}\,\bigl(C_{J}+E_{J}+G_{J}+I_{J}+C_{D}+E_{D}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{\bar{d}^{2}\log\bar{d}}{\bar{d}^{2}-1}\,\bigl(B_{J}+E_{J}+H_{J}+I_{J}+B_{D}+E_{D}\bigr)+\Big(\log d+\log\bar{d}\Big)\Big(A_{J}+B_{J}+C_{J}

\displaystyle+E_{J}+F_{J}+G_{J}+H_{J}+I_{J}+A_{D}+B_{D}+C_{D}+E_{D}\Big)\Biggr].

(219)

Since correction of
S\big(\sigma^{(\epsilon)}_{A_{1}\bar{A}_{1}}\big)
vanishes, the relevant part of the coherent information needed to determine the PA entropy is given by

-\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
. We therefore focus on analyzing and minimizing
\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
.

Since
\log\!\big((1-3\Delta)/\Delta\big)
diverges as
\Delta\to 0^{+}
, the coherent-information correction becomes singular in the regulator-removal limit. We therefore keep
\Delta
fixed and use the coherent information as a regulated comparison functional for different recovery choices. Maximizing the coherent information over
R_{A}\otimes R_{\bar{A}}
then provides a well-defined notion of optimal recovery at fixed
\Delta
, as discussed in Sec. (
A.3
).

In our expression for
\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
, the recovery dependence enters only through the coefficients appearing in the square brackets in Eq. (
A.5
). All remaining prefactors are independent of the recovery choice.

Among the coefficients entering the PA entropy (
178
) through
\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
, namely
C_{J}
,
E_{J}
,
B_{D}
,
C_{D}
, and
E_{D}
, only
C_{J}
is affected by the boundary-unitary freedom in the recovery.
To see this, let us examine how the boundary-unitary freedom acts on the operators

J
and
D
. A redefinition of the recovery of the form

R_{A}^{(\epsilon)}\;\longrightarrow\;e^{i\epsilon O_{A}}\,R_{A}^{(\epsilon)},\qquad R_{\bar{A}}^{(\epsilon)}\;\longrightarrow\;e^{i\epsilon O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}}\,R_{\bar{A}}^{(\epsilon)},

(220)

with Hermitian
O_{A}
on
A=A_{1}\otimes A_{2}
and Hermitian
O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}
on

\bar{A}=\bar{A}_{1}\otimes\bar{A}_{2}
, induces, to first order in
\epsilon
, the shift

W_{R}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{R}+O_{A}\otimes I_{\bar{A}}+I_{A}\otimes O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}.

(221)

Using Eq (
153
), the corresponding variations are

\displaystyle\delta J

\displaystyle=d_{\chi}\,\Tr_{\bar{A}_{2}}\!\Bigl(\{O_{A}\otimes I_{\bar{A}},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\}+\{I_{A}\otimes O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}\}\Bigr),

(222)

\displaystyle\delta D

\displaystyle=id_{\chi}\,\Tr_{\bar{A}_{2}}\!\Bigl([O_{A}\otimes I_{\bar{A}},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}]+[I_{A}\otimes O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}},\chi_{A_{2}\bar{A}_{2}}]\Bigr).

(223)

We now expand
J
and
D
in the same Pauli basis as in
Eq. (
159
),

X=\sum_{l=0}^{d^{2}-1}\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{d}^{2}-1}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{\chi}^{2}-1}p_{lmn}(X)\,P_{l}^{(A_{1})}\otimes P_{m}^{(\bar{A}_{1})}\otimes P_{n}^{(A_{2})},\qquad X\in\{J,D\}.

(224)

From (
222
) and (
223
), one sees that the
shift generated by
O_{A}
is supported only in the sector with
m=0
, while
the shift generated by
O^{\prime}_{\bar{A}}
is supported only in the sector with

l=0
. Hence the coefficients with

l>0,\qquad m>0

are untouched by the boundary-unitary freedom. Equivalently,

\delta p_{lmn}(X)=0,\qquad X\in\{J,D\},\,\text{for}\quad l>0,\quad m>0.

On the other hand, coefficients in the sectors with either
m=0
or
l=0
can be affected by the transformation (
220
).
This immediately explains the behavior of the grouped coefficients appearing in

\mathscr{S}_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
.Hence, the quantities

\displaystyle E_{J}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,m,n>0}p_{lmn}^{2}(J),\qquad B_{D}=\sum_{m,n>0}p_{0mn}^{2}(D),

(225)

\displaystyle C_{D}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,n>0}p_{l0n}^{2}(D),\qquad E_{D}=\sum_{l,m,n>0}p_{lmn}^{2}(D)

are invariant under the local-unitary freedom relevant here, whereas

C_{J}=\sum_{l,n>0}p_{l0n}^{2}(J),\qquad\text{and}\qquad B_{J}=\sum_{m,n>0}p_{0mn}^{2}(J)

(226)

lies precisely in the
m=0
and
l=0
sector of
J
and therefore can change under the
shift generated by
O_{A}
and
O_{\bar{A}}^{\prime}
. In fact, one can show that by proper choice of
O_{A}
and
O_{\bar{A}}^{\prime}
, the two coefficients
C_{J}
and
B_{J}
can be set to zero. Since both of them enter Eq. (
A.5
) as a positive contribution. This choice optimizes the coherent information.

From the result in Sec.
A.4
, the PA entropy depends on
C_{J}
, but not on
B_{J}
, and all other contributing terms remain fixed uncer the local transformation. Therefore we conclude that the optimization condition sets

C_{J}=0\implies k_{1}=0,

(227)

∎

A.6
Theorem
4.4

Proof.

First we show that approximate Knill-Laflamme (aKL) condition Eq. (
35
) implies the skewing condition Eq. (
36
).

To make the logical tensor-product structure explicit, choose a product basis

\{\ket{i}_{a}\}_{i=1}^{d}
of
\mathcal{L}_{a}
and
\{\ket{j}_{\bar{a}}\}_{j=1}^{\bar{d}}
of
\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}}
,
and define the corresponding code basis

\ket{\widetilde{ij}}\;:=\;V\bigl(\ket{i}_{a}\otimes\ket{j}_{\bar{a}}\bigr)\in\mathcal{C}.

Now restrict to operators supported on
\bar{A}
. Let
\{P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma}\}_{\gamma=0}^{d_{\bar{A}}^{2}-1}
be any operator basis of

\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}})
(for concreteness one may take the Pauli basis when
\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}}
is a qubit system). Then the

\bar{A}
-part of the aKL condition (
35
) is equivalently the collection of matrix-element identities

\bra{\widetilde{ij}}\,(I_{A}\otimes P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma})\,\ket{\widetilde{kl}}\;=\;\delta_{ik}\,\bra{j}\,\mathbb{P}_{\bar{a}}^{\gamma}\,\ket{l}\;+\;\epsilon\,\bar{Y}^{\gamma}_{ij;kl},

(228)

where we have set

\mathbb{P}_{\bar{a}}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}(P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma})\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}}),\qquad\bar{Y}^{\gamma}_{ij;kl}:=\bar{Y}_{ij;kl}(P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma}).

We adopt the convention
P_{\bar{A}}^{0}=I_{\bar{A}}
, so that

\mathbb{P}_{\bar{a}}^{0}=\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}(I_{\bar{A}})=I_{\bar{a}}
and
\bar{Y}^{0}_{ij;kl}=0
.

Consider the Choi state associated with the encoding isometry
V
,

\ket{\phi}\;:=\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{L}}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{d}}\ket{ij}_{r\bar{r}}\,\ket{\widetilde{ij}}_{A\bar{A}},

(229)

where we have written
d:=\dim\mathcal{L}_{a}
,
\bar{d}:=\dim\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}}
so that
d_{L}=d\,\bar{d}
, and where
r,\bar{r}
are reference systems
isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{a},\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}}
, respectively.

Tracing out
A
yields a state on
r\bar{r}\bar{A}
:

\displaystyle\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}

\displaystyle:=\Tr_{A}\!\left(\ket{\phi}\bra{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\,I_{r}\otimes\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}\;+\;\epsilon\,\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}.

(230)

The first term captures the ideal decoupling of
r
from
\bar{r}\bar{A}
, while
\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
collects the deviation.

Using the matrix-element identity (
228
), we can express
\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}
and
\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}

in an operator basis. Let
\{P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma}\}_{\gamma}
be an orthonormal operator basis on
\bar{A}

(e.g. Pauli operators for qubit systems), and let
\{\mathbb{P}^{\gamma}_{\bar{r}}\}_{\gamma}
denote the corresponding operators on

\bar{r}
defined by the aKL map (i.e.
\mathbb{P}^{\gamma}_{\bar{r}}
is the representation of
\mathbb{P}^{\gamma}_{\bar{a}}
on
\bar{r}

under the fixed identification
\bar{r}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}}
). Then

\displaystyle\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{\bar{A}}\,\bar{d}}\sum_{\gamma}\mathbb{P}^{\gamma}_{\bar{r}}\otimes P^{\gamma}_{\bar{A}},

(231)

\displaystyle\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{L}\,d_{\bar{A}}}\sum_{\gamma}\bar{Y}^{\gamma}_{r\bar{r}}\otimes P^{\gamma}_{\bar{A}},\qquad\bar{Y}^{\gamma}_{r\bar{r}}:=\frac{1}{d_{L}}\sum_{\beta}\Tr\!\bigl(\bar{Y}^{\gamma}P^{\beta}\bigr)\,P^{\beta}_{r\bar{r}}.

(232)

Here
\{P^{\beta}_{r\bar{r}}\}_{\beta}
is an orthonormal operator basis on
r\bar{r}
(again, Pauli strings in the qubit case),
and
\bar{Y}^{\gamma}
denotes the matrix with entries
\bar{Y}^{\gamma}_{ij;kl}
in the
\ket{ij}
basis.

By Choi’s theorem, a linear map
\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}:\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\bar{A}})\to\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}})
is completely positive
if and only if its Choi operator is positive semidefinite. Equivalently,

\displaystyle J(\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{d_{\bar{A}}}\sum_{m,n=1}^{d_{\bar{A}}}\ket{m}\!\bra{n}_{\bar{A}}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}\!\left(\ket{m}\!\bra{n}_{\bar{A}}\right)\succeq 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{\bar{A}}}\sum_{\gamma}P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{\bar{a}}^{\gamma}\succeq 0,

(233)

where in the second line we expanded the maximally entangled operator

\sum_{m,n}\ket{m}\!\bra{n}\otimes\ket{m}\!\bra{n}
in the operator basis
\{P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma}\}_{\gamma}
, and
used
\mathbb{P}_{\bar{a}}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}(P_{\bar{A}}^{\gamma})
.

Under the fixed identification
\bar{r}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\bar{a}}
, the operator
\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}
defined in
(
231
) coincides (up to relabeling) with the normalized Choi operator of
\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}
.
Since
\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}
is completely positive, its Choi operator is positive semi-definite, and hence

\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}\succeq 0.

Moreover, with the convention
P_{\bar{A}}^{0}=I_{\bar{A}}
and

\mathbb{P}_{\bar{a}}^{0}=\mathcal{E}_{\bar{A}}(I_{\bar{A}})=I_{\bar{a}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Tr(\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{\bar{A}}\,\bar{d}}\Tr\!\left(\mathbb{P}_{\bar{r}}^{0}\otimes P_{\bar{A}}^{0}\right)=\frac{1}{d_{\bar{A}}\,\bar{d}}\,\Tr(I_{\bar{r}})\Tr(I_{\bar{A}})=1,

(234)

so
\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}
is a valid density operator.

Any density operator admits a purification on an complement space of dimension at least its rank.
We therefore fix a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}}
with

\dim\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}}\;\geq\;\rank(\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}),

(235)

and choose a purification
\ket{\xi}_{A_{2}\bar{r}\bar{A}}
such that

\Tr_{A_{2}}\ket{\xi}\bra{\xi}=\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}.

(236)

Define the product state

\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}:=\frac{1}{d}I_{r}\otimes\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}},

which is the leading-order term in (
230
). A convenient purification of
\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
on

rA_{1}A_{2}\bar{r}\bar{A}
is

\ket{\phi_{0}^{\prime}}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\ket{i}_{r}\otimes\ket{i}_{A_{1}}\otimes\ket{\xi}_{A_{2}\bar{r}\bar{A}},

(237)

where we used
\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\simeq r
to purify
\frac{1}{d}I_{r}
by the maximally entangled state

\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{i}\ket{i}_{r}\ket{i}_{A_{1}}
.

If the physical subsystem
A
is large enough to accommodate the purification
\ket{\phi_{0}^{\prime}}

i.e. if

d\,\rank\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}}\leq d_{A},

(238)

then we choose
\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}}
such that the
\mathcal{H}_{A}
has the following decomposition,

\mathcal{H}_{A}\simeq(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}})\oplus\mathcal{H}_{A_{3}}.

(239)

Otherwise, if (
238
) fails, we adjoin an auxiliary system
E
initialized in
\ket{0}_{E}
and work in

\mathcal{H}_{AE}=\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{E}
, choosing
\mathcal{H}_{E}
large enough so that

d\,\dim\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}}\leq d_{A}\,d_{E}.

(240)

In this case we obtain the analogous decomposition

\mathcal{H}_{AE}\simeq(\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}})\oplus\mathcal{H}_{A_{3}}.

(241)

In what follows we write
AE
for the purifying register, with the understanding that the special case
d_{E}=1
corresponds to
no ancilla (for the case (
238
) holds).

Using the standard inequalities

1-\sqrt{F(\sigma,\tau)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\sigma-\tau\|_{1}\leq\frac{\sqrt{D}}{2}\|\sigma-\tau\|_{2},\qquad D:=\dim(\mathrm{supp}),

and noting that
\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
and
\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
act on a space of dimension
D=d_{L}\,d_{\bar{A}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle 1-\sqrt{F(\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}},\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{d_{L}d_{\bar{A}}}}{2}\,\|\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}-\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}\|

\displaystyle=\epsilon\,\frac{\sqrt{d_{L}d_{\bar{A}}}}{2}\,\sqrt{\Tr(\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}^{\dagger})}.

(242)

Substituting the expansion (
232
) of
\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
and using orthonormality of the operator basis on
\bar{A}

gives

\displaystyle\Tr(\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}\eta_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{L}^{2}d_{\bar{A}}}\sum_{\gamma=0}^{d_{\bar{A}}^{2}-1}\Tr\!\left(\bar{Y}^{\gamma}\bar{Y}^{\gamma\dagger}\right),

(243)

and therefore

1-\sqrt{F(\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}},\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}})}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{d_{L}}}\,\Bigl(\sum_{\gamma}\Tr(\bar{Y}^{\gamma}\bar{Y}^{\gamma\dagger})\Bigr)^{1/2}=\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{d_{L}}}\,\|\bar{Y}\|,

(244)

where

\|\bar{Y}\|:=\left(\sum_{\gamma=0}^{d_{\bar{A}}^{2}-1}\Tr(\bar{Y}^{\gamma}\bar{Y}^{\gamma\dagger})\right)^{1/2}.

By Uhlmann’s theorem, there exists a purification
\ket{\phi_{0}}
of
\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
on the purifying register
AE
such that

F(\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}},\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}})=\bigl|\bra{\phi,0_{E}}\phi_{0}\rangle\bigr|^{2},\qquad\Tr_{AE}\ket{\phi_{0}}\bra{\phi_{0}}=\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}},

(245)

and this overlap is maximal over all purifications of
\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
on
AE
.
Combining with (
244
) yields

\displaystyle\|\ket{\phi}\ket{0}_{E}-\ket{\phi_{0}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=2-2\Re\braket{\phi,0_{E}|\phi_{0}}=2\bigl(1-\sqrt{F(\phi_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}},\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}})}\bigr)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{d_{L}}}\,\|\bar{Y}\|,

(246)

hence

\|\ket{\phi}\ket{0}_{E}-\ket{\phi_{0}}\|\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{d_{L}}}\,\|\bar{Y}\|\right)^{1/2}.

(247)

Finally, since
\ket{\phi_{0}}
and
\ket{\phi_{0}^{\prime}}
are both purifications of
\rho_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}}
, they differ by a unitary on the
purifying register
AE
. That is, there exists a unitary
R_{AE}
on
AE
such that

\ket{\phi_{0}}=(R_{AE}^{\dagger}\otimes I_{r\bar{r}\bar{A}})\ket{\phi_{0}^{\prime}}.

(248)

Plugging this into (
247
), we may write

\ket{\phi}\ket{0}_{E}=R_{AE}^{\dagger}\ket{\phi_{0}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{N}(\epsilon)\ket{\delta},

(249)

for some unit vector
\ket{\delta}
, where the error amplitude obeys

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon)=\bigl\|\ket{\phi}\ket{0}_{E}-R_{AE}^{\dagger}\ket{\phi_{0}^{\prime}}\bigr\|\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{d_{L}}}\,\|\bar{Y}\|\right)^{1/2}.

Since
\rho_{\bar{r}}:=\Tr_{\bar{A}}(\rho_{\bar{r}\bar{A}})=\frac{1}{\bar{d}}I_{\bar{r}}
has a flat spectrum, any purification
